AssistantMessage(content=[TextBlock(text='The build succeeds and the fix is in place.\n\n## Diagnosis\n\nThe crash is an `ArithmeticException: divide by zero` in `TotpProgressBar.refresh()`:\n\n```java\nint maxProgress = getMax();          // 5000 (card_entry.xml) or 1000 (compact/small layouts)\nint animPart = maxProgress / _period;  // 5000 / 9999 == 0\nint animEnd = (currentProgress / animPart) * animPart;   // ← / by zero\n```\n\nThe animation was written assuming the progress bar\'s `max` is a multiple of the period, i.e. that one second of the period is worth at least one unit of progress. `animPart` is "progress units per second" as *integer* division, so for any period larger than the bar\'s `max` (>1000 in the compact/small entry layouts, >5000 in the default one) it becomes `0` and the next line throws.\n\n`setShowProgress()` → `_progressBar.start()` → `refresh()` runs on the UI thread while binding the entry, so the app crashed instantly and then crashed again on every launch once the 9999s entry was saved in the vault — exactly what the reporter described.\n\n## Fix\n\nReworked `refresh()` to do the chunking in the **time domain** rather than in integer progress units, so it is period-independent:\n\n- The chunk length is `clamp(1s … millisTillRotation)` but never shorter than the time it takes the bar to advance by one unit (`periodMillis / maxProgress`). This removes the `animPart` division entirely, and — importantly — also prevents the degenerate case where a naive `Math.max(animPart, 1)` guard would make `animEnd == currentProgress`, yielding a `postDelayed(…, 0)` busy-loop / ANR instead of a crash.\n- `animEnd` is computed as the progress value at `now + chunk`, via a shared `calcProgress()` helper that clamps to `[0, max]` and uses `double`/`long` math (the old `float` + `_period * 1000` int expression was also overflow-prone).\n- Guarded the `1000 / _animDurationScale` division against a scale of `0` (animations disabled in developer options), which previously produced a `Integer.MAX_VALUE` duration.\n- `setPeriod()` now falls back to the default 30s for periods `TotpInfo.isPeriodValid()` rejects, and the static `TotpInfo.getMillisTillNextRotation(int)` does the same — it is called with `EntryAdapter.getMostFrequentPeriod()`, which returns `-1` when there is no uniform period group and previously produced a *negative* delay fed straight into `UiRefresher.postDelayed()`.\n\nNote the period `9999` remains valid and usable, which matches the reporter\'s expectation ("new code should be generated when the entered time is up") rather than rejecting the input.\n\nI verified the arithmetic by extracting the old and new math into a standalone harness and sweeping periods `{1, 5, 30, 60, 999, 1000, 1001, 9999, 2147483, 0, -1}` × max `{1000, 5000}`, stepping through a full rotation each time. The old code throws `/ by zero` for every period exceeding `max` (and for `0`) and yields negative progress for `-1`; the new code produces in-range, non-increasing progress with strictly positive re-post delays in all cases, and is byte-for-byte equivalent in behaviour for the common 30s/60s periods.\n\n## Patch\n\n```diff\n--- a/app/src/main/java/com/beemdevelopment/aegis/otp/TotpInfo.java\n+++ b/app/src/main/java/com/beemdevelopment/aegis/otp/TotpInfo.java\n@@ -12,11 +12,13 @@ import java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n public class TotpInfo extends OtpInfo {\n     public static final String ID = "totp";\n \n+    public static final int DEFAULT_PERIOD = 30;\n+\n     private int _period;\n \n     public TotpInfo(byte[] secret) throws OtpInfoException {\n         super(secret);\n-        setPeriod(30);\n+        setPeriod(DEFAULT_PERIOD);\n     }\n \n@@ -75,8 +77,15 @@ public class TotpInfo extends OtpInfo {\n     public static long getMillisTillNextRotation(int period) {\n-        long p = period * 1000;\n+        if (!isPeriodValid(period)) {\n+            // this method is also called with the period of a group of entries, which may\n+            // be invalid if there is no such group. fall back to the default period to\n+            // prevent returning a bogus (negative) amount of time\n+            period = DEFAULT_PERIOD;\n+        }\n+\n+        long p = period * 1000L;\n         return p -
